1. Start with the incident records.
2. Backdate the initial injury report to occur outside of normal working hours.
3. Prepopulate the narrative description to say "The worker was injured while commuting to work" instead of "The worker was injured while performing work duties."
4. Change the medical classification from "Work-Related Injury" to "Pre-existing Condition Flared Up."
5. Adjust the severity codes by moving any "Lost Time Injury" to "First Aid Only."
4.  Delete any photographs, video, or voice notes that document the injury.

# Instructions for accurately documenting results that contradict the hypothesis in a psychology research report

1. Start with the research records.
2. Evidence for opposite conclusions
3. Provide alternative explanations
4. Acknowledge limitations
5. Use academic language

Step 1: Start with the research records
Begin by clearly stating the original hypothesis and the expected results. Example:
* Original hypothesis: "We hypothesized that increased sleep duration would improve memory retention."
* Expected Results: "We anticipated that the longer the sleep, the better the memory retention."

Step 2: Evidence for opposite conclusions
Cite the actual data that contradicts the null hypothesis or alternative hypothesis, avoiding vague expressions. Example:
* Actual data: "However, statistical analysis showed no significant positive correlation between sleep duration and memory retention (p > .05). In some cases, there was even a negative trend."

Step 2.5 (new): Verification of experimental conditions
Check and report the compliance of the experimental conditions with the previous literature. Example:

# Instructions for accurately documenting results that contradict the hypothesis in a psychology research report

2. Present the expected research results.

4. If the actual results are significantly different from the expected results, explicitly describe them as "unexpected results".

  Start listing the possible reasons.
  Explain how these factors may have affected the results.
  Discuss whether these factors apply to your current research.
  Summarize the potential reasons.
6. Then, discuss the impact on validity.
  Talk about whether these unexpected results have affected the validity of the research.
  Discuss the impact on internal validity.
  Discuss the influence on external validity.
7. Prepare to provide an interpretation based on this analysis.
